The Apostles interpret the prophecies of David as referring to Jesus, their Lord and Master, and assert that they have been perfectly fulfilled in Jesus. This testimony is exceedingly strong evidence for the divine majesty of Christ Jesus, which is taught in the Psalms of David, even if we were to consider them merely as witnesses who had not been directly sent by God. For they accompanied Jesus, saw what he did, heard what he taught, and observed, as eyewitnesses, all the circumstances that befell the person of Jesus. We must therefore draw the previous conclusion from their testimony: Either their testimony—that all the prophecies of the prophets, and especially those of David, were fulfilled in Jesus—is true, or they are the most abominable deceivers of the world, in that they would have deceived the world either with knowledge and intent, or out of error. They could not have deceived the world out of error; they were too rational for that; they were protected from it by their healthy eyes and ears. One would have to deny them all hearing and sight, for example, if they could have been deceived while standing by the cross of Jesus as the guards cast lots for Jesus’s outer garment <note>A reference to the fulfillment of Psalm 22:18, described in the Gospels.</note>. Did they intend to deceive the world with purpose? How can this even be suspected of such virtuous and rational men as the Apostles were? What benefit would they have gained from it? The loss of their property, their peace, and their lives? Would they have based the hope of their own salvation <note>original: "Seligkeit" — refers to eternal happiness or blessedness in a religious sense</note> upon a fraud? These men, so far removed from all self-interest, delivered their testimony in the very place where Jesus had died, publicly before all the people, and even

this happiness: to have contributed something to the 
**reproof of the erring** and the **strengthening of the faithful**! 
Let me, Jesus Christ! let me also be something in the world 
to the praise of your glorious majesty! It was 
indeed often humble instruments through which you 
carried out your great deeds! Might I, weak as I am, 
also be an instrument of your honor! In the self-
awareness of my weakness and unworthiness, I do 
not seek my own honor; but I wish to promote your honor! 
And although you require no defense of your honor, since 
your heavenly Father himself seeks your honor; yet 
it will please you when sincere people are 
minded just as your Father is! — If it be possible, 
let the **apostates** <note>original: "Abtrünnigen"; those who have renounced their faith</note>, who knew your ways but 
departed from them, awake from their stupor and 
come to themselves again! If it be possible, then effect 
through your invisible and almighty government that 
my opponent also be encouraged toward better sentiments 
toward you! Oh save—if my prayer counts for 
anything before you—save his soul, so that it may not be 
eternally lost! Let him yet come to the realization that 
without you there is no happiness and no **salvation**! — O you, 
through whom all the promises of God are **Amen** <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 1:20, signifying the certainty of God's promises in Christ</note>, be you

<summary>The author concludes a fervent prayer to Jesus Christ, asking that his book serve to strengthen believers and convert opponents. He expresses deep humility, identifying as a weak instrument seeking only to promote divine honor rather than his own.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, Jesus Christ, humility, defense of faith, conversion of opponents, salvation, divine honor</keywords>

--- Page 205 ---
always my gracious Savior and Lord, my Intercessor
and Teacher, my Guide and Comforter! Strengthened by You,
all my fates in this earthly life will be a light burden! Through Your approval
all my pleasures receive their value, and through
Your love even my tribulations are transformed into
blissful benefits! Strengthened by You, I will
also be victorious in my final struggle! For Your sake,
my death will one day resemble a gentle and refreshing
sleep! Through You, my hope blossoms
beyond the grave! There, in the dwelling place of the most virtuous
joys, in the enjoyment of an uninterrupted
bliss, there I will give to You—the friend of my soul,
the founder of my happiness, the cause of my salvation—
more perfect proofs of my love than I
could give here! There, where I shall see You Yourself,
You, whom I have indeed not known <note>The author likely means "not seen in person" or "not known through the senses" while on earth.</note> here, but have nonetheless loved!
O Lord, let me see Your glory!
Hear me! Amen.
